**Speaker:** Narrator · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Transmission to later generations, Restoration after loss · **Object:** the book of the law of Moses

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Ezra 7:6 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">הוּא עֶזְרָא עָלָה מִבָּבֶל וְהֽוּא־סֹפֵר מָהִיר בְּתוֹרַת מֹשֶׁה אֲשֶׁר־נָתַן יְהוָה אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל וַיִּתֶּן־לוֹ הַמֶּלֶךְ כְּיַד־יְהוָה אֱלֹהָיו עָלָיו כֹּל בַּקָּשָׁתֽוֹ׃</span>
>
> Ezra 7:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּי עֶזְרָא הֵכִין לְבָבוֹ לִדְרוֹשׁ אֶת־תּוֹרַת יְהוָה וְלַעֲשֹׂת וּלְלַמֵּד בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל חֹק וּמִשְׁפָּֽט׃</span>
>
> Nehemiah 8:1 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיֵּאָסְפוּ כָל־הָעָם כְּאִישׁ אֶחָד אֶל־הָרְחוֹב אֲשֶׁר לִפְנֵי שַֽׁעַר־הַמָּיִם וַיֹּֽאמְרוּ לְעֶזְרָא הַסֹּפֵר לְהָבִיא אֶת־סֵפֶר תּוֹרַת מֹשֶׁה אֲשֶׁר־צִוָּה יְהוָה אֶת־יִשְׂרָאֵֽל׃</span>
>
> Nehemiah 8:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיָּבִיא עֶזְרָא הַכֹּהֵן אֶֽת־הַתּוֹרָה לִפְנֵי הַקָּהָל מֵאִישׁ וְעַד־אִשָּׁה וְכֹל מֵבִין לִשְׁמֹעַ בְּיוֹם אֶחָד לַחֹדֶשׁ הַשְּׁבִיעִֽי׃</span>
>
> Nehemiah 8:3 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיִּקְרָא־בוֹ לִפְנֵי הָרְחוֹב אֲשֶׁר ׀ לִפְנֵי שַֽׁעַר־הַמַּיִם מִן־הָאוֹר עַד־מַחֲצִית הַיּוֹם נֶגֶד הָאֲנָשִׁים וְהַנָּשִׁים וְהַמְּבִינִים וְאָזְנֵי כָל־הָעָם אֶל־סֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָֽה׃</span>
>
> Nehemiah 8:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַֽיִּקְרְאוּ בַסֵּפֶר בְּתוֹרַת הָאֱלֹהִים מְפֹרָשׁ וְשׂוֹם שֶׂכֶל וַיָּבִינוּ בַּמִּקְרָֽא׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Ezra 7:6 — This Ezra went up from Babylon; and he [was] a ready scribe in the law of Moses, which the LORD God of Israel had given: and the king granted him all his request, according to the hand of the LORD his God upon him.
>
> Ezra 7:10 — For Ezra had prepared his heart to seek the law of the LORD, and to do [it], and to teach in Israel statutes and judgments.
>

>
> Nehemiah 8:2 — And Ezra the priest brought the law before the congregation both of men and women, and all that could hear with understanding, upon the first day of the seventh month.
>
> Nehemiah 8:3 — And he read therein before the street that [was] before the water gate from the morning until midday, before the men and the women, and those that could understand; and the ears of all the people [were attentive] unto the book of the law.
>
> Nehemiah 8:8 — So they read in the book in the law of God distinctly, and gave the sense, and caused [them] to understand the reading.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Ezra 7:6 — Αὐτὸς Ἔσδρας ἀνέβη ἐκ Βαβυλῶνος, καὶ αὐτὸς γραμματεὺς ταχὺς ἐν νόμῳ Μωυσῆ, ὃν ἔδωκε Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς Ἰσραήλ· καὶ ἔδωκεν αὐτῷ ὁ βασιλεὺς, ὅτι χεὶρ Κυρίου Θεοῦ αὐτοῦ ἐπʼ αὐτὸν ἐν πᾶσιν οἷς ἐζήτει αὐτός.
>
> Ezra 7:10 — ὅτι Ἔσδρας ἔδωκεν ἐν καρδίᾳ αὐτοῦ ζητῆσαι τὸν νόμον, καὶ ποιεῖν καὶ διδάσκειν ἐν Ἰσραὴλ προστάγματα καὶ κρίματα.
>
> Ezra 18:1 — Καὶ ἔφθασεν ὁ μὴν ὁ ἕβδομος, καὶ οἱ υἱοὶ Ἰσραὴλ ἐν πόλεσιν αὐτῶν· καὶ συνήχθησαν πᾶς ὁ λαὸς ὡς ἀνὴρ εἷς εἰς τὸ πλάτος τὸ ἔμπροσθεν πύλης τοῦ ὕδατος· καὶ εἶπαν τῷ Ἔσδρᾳ τῷ γραμματεῖ, ἐνέγκαι τὸ βιβλίον νόμου Μωυσῆ, ὃν ἐνετείλατο Κύριος τῷ Ἰσραήλ.
>
> Ezra 18:2 — Καὶ ἤνεγκεν Ἔσδρας ὁ ἱερεὺς τὸν νόμον ἐνώπιον τῆς ἐκκλησίας ἀπὸ ἀνδρὸς ἕως γυναικὸς, καὶ πᾶς ὁ συνιὼν, ἀκούειν ἐν ἡμέρᾳ μιᾷ τοῦ μηνὸς τοῦ ἑβδόμου.
>
> Ezra 18:3 — Καὶ ἀνέγνω ἐν αὐτῷ ἀπὸ τῆς ὥρας τοῦ διαφωτίσαι τὸν ἥλιον ἕως ἡμίσους τῆς ἡμέρας, ἀπέναντι τῶν ἀνδρῶν καὶ τῶν γυναικῶν, καὶ αὐτοὶ συνιέντες· καὶ ὦτα παντὸς τοῦ λαοῦ εἰς τὸ βιβλίον τοῦ νόμου.
>
> Ezra 18:8 — Καὶ ἀνέγνωσαν ἐν βιβλίῳ νόμου τοῦ Θεοῦ, καὶ ἐδίδασκεν Ἔσδρας, καὶ διέστελλεν ἐν ἐπιστήμῃ Κυρίου, καὶ συνῆκεν ὁ λαὸς ἐν τῇ ἀναγνώσει.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Ezra 7:6 — This Esdras went up out of Babylon; and he was a ready scribe in the law of Moses, which the Lord God of Israel gave: and the king gave him [leave], for the hand of the Lord his God was upon him in all things which he sought.
>
> Ezra 7:10 — For Esdras had determined in his heart to seek the law, and to do and teach the ordinances and judgments in Israel.
>
> Ezra 18:1 — And the seventh month arrived, and the children of Israel [were settled] in their cities; and all the people were gathered as one man to the broad place before the water-gate, and they told Esdras the scribe to bring the book of the law of Moses, which the Lord commanded Israel.
>
> Ezra 18:2 — So Esdras the priest brought the law before the congregation both of men and women, and every one who had understanding [was present] to hearken, on the first day of the seventh month.
>
> Ezra 18:3 — And he read in it from the time of sun-rise to the middle of the day, before the men and the women; and they understood [it], and the ears of all the people [were attentive] to the book of the law.
>
> Ezra 18:8 — And they read in the book of the law of God, and Esdras taught, and instructed them distinctly in the knowledge of the Lord, and the people understood [the law] in the reading.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Greek numbers Nehemiah as chapters 11-23 of 2 Esdras (Brenton's EZR),
so Nehemiah 8 is 18. In Nehemiah 8:8 it reads "and Esdras taught, and
instructed them distinctly in the knowledge of the Lord" for "distinctly,
and gave the sense".

**Notes.**

After the exile the law is brought out by a scribe, read publicly and
explained. **[inferred]** The scene presupposes that the book of the law
of Moses survived the destruction of the temple and the exile, though
Scripture does not say how.

### Nehemiah 9:8

<a id="neh-9-8"></a>

**Claim.** Thou "hast performed thy words; for thou art righteous".

**Speaker:** Another speaker · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** thy words to Abraham

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Nehemiah 9:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וּמָצָאתָ אֶת־לְבָבוֹ נֶאֱמָן לְפָנֶיךָ וְכָרוֹת עִמּוֹ הַבְּרִית לָתֵת אֶת־אֶרֶץ הַכְּנַעֲנִי הַחִתִּי הָאֱמֹרִי וְהַפְּרִזִּי וְהַיְבוּסִי וְהַגִּרְגָּשִׁי לָתֵת לְזַרְעוֹ וַתָּקֶם אֶת־דְּבָרֶיךָ כִּי צַדִּיק אָֽתָּה׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Nehemiah 9:8 — And foundest his heart faithful before thee, and madest a covenant with him to give the land of the Canaanites, the Hittites, the Amorites, and the Perizzites, and the Jebusites, and the Girgashites, to give [it, I say], to his seed, and hast performed thy words; for thou [art] righteous:

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Ezra 19:8 — Καὶ εὗρες τὴν καρδίαν αὐτοῦ πιστὴν ἐνώπιόν σου, καὶ διέθου πρὸς αὐτὸν διαθήκην δοῦναι αὐτῷ τὴν γῆν τῶν Χαναναίων, καὶ Χετταίων, καὶ Ἀμοῤῥαίων, καὶ Φερεζαίων, καὶ Ἰεβουσαίων, καὶ Γεργεσαίων, καὶ τῷ σπέρματι αὐτοῦ· καὶ ἔστησας τοὺς λόγους σου, ὅτι δίκαιος σύ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Ezra 19:8 — and thou foundest his heart faithful before thee, and didst make a covenant with him to give to him and to his seed the land of the Chananites, and the Chettites, and Amorites, and Pherezites, and Jebusites, and Gergesites; and thou hast confirmed thy words, for thou [art] righteous.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "performed" is ἔστησας, "established".

**Notes.**

The Levites' prayer. God's keeping of his word is grounded in his righteousness.

### Psalm 12:6-7

<a id="ps-12-6"></a>

**Claim.** The words of the LORD are pure words, silver tried seven times. Thou shalt keep them, O LORD, thou shalt preserve them from this generation for ever.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Purity and truth, Transmission to later generations · **Object:** the words of the LORD; and "them" (contested)

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 12:7 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אִֽמֲרוֹת יְהוָה אֲמָרוֹת טְהֹרוֹת כֶּסֶף צָרוּף בַּעֲלִיל לָאָרֶץ מְזֻקָּק שִׁבְעָתָֽיִם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 12:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אַתָּֽה־יְהוָה תִּשְׁמְרֵם תִּצְּרֶנּוּ ׀ מִן־הַדּוֹר זוּ לְעוֹלָֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 12:6 — The words of the LORD [are] pure words: [as] silver tried in a furnace of earth, purified seven times.
>
> Psalm 12:7 — Thou shalt keep them, O LORD, thou shalt preserve them from this generation for ever.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 11:7 — Τὰ λόγια Κυρίου, λόγια ἁγνά· ἀργύριον πεπυρωμένον, δοκίμιον τῇ γῇ, κεκαθαρισμένον ἑπταπλασίως.
>
> Psalm 11:8 — Σὺ Κύριε φυλάξεις ἡμᾶς· καὶ διατηρήσεις ἡμᾶς ἀπὸ τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης, καὶ εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 11:7 — The oracles of the Lord are pure oracles; as silver tried in the fire, proved [in] a furnace of earth, purified seven times.
>
> Psalm 11:8 — Thou, O Lord, shalt keep us, and shalt preserve us, from this generation, and for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The sharpest contrast in the collection.

- **Hebrew** (12:8): אַתָּה־יְהוָה תִּשְׁמְרֵם תִּצְּרֶנּוּ — "Thou, LORD,
  wilt keep *them*; thou wilt preserve *him* (or *it*)." The first suffix
  is masculine plural; the second is singular.
- **Greek** (11:8): Σὺ Κύριε φυλάξεις ἡμᾶς καὶ διατηρήσεις ἡμᾶς — "Thou,
  O Lord, shalt keep *us*, and shalt preserve *us*."

The Greek therefore makes the psalm a prayer for the preservation of the
faithful, not of the words. In the Hebrew, "words" (אִמֲרוֹת, v. 7) is
feminine plural, and the masculine "them" more naturally takes up "the
poor" and "the needy" of verse 6 (5 in English), whom God has just
promised to set in safety; but Hebrew does not always keep gender
agreement, which is why the verse has also been read of the words.
**[inferred]** The
grammar favours the poor; the Greek takes the same line, with a
first-person "us".

**Notes.**

The Westminster Larger Catechism (Q. 4) cites Psalm 12:6 for the
"purity" of Scripture, meaning its content, not its transmission. Verse 6
(7 in the Hebrew) itself is about purity, not preservation.

<a id="ps-19-9"></a>

**Claim.** The fear of the LORD is clean, enduring for ever; the judgments of the LORD are true.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence, Purity and truth · **Object:** the fear of the LORD (here a name for his law)

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 19:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">יִרְאַת יְהוָה ׀ טְהוֹרָה עוֹמֶדֶת לָעַד מִֽשְׁפְּטֵי־יְהוָה אֱמֶת צָֽדְקוּ יַחְדָּֽו׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 19:9 — The fear of the LORD [is] clean, enduring for ever: the judgments of the LORD [are] true [and] righteous altogether.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 18:10 — Ὁ φόβος Κυρίου ἁγνὸς διαμένων εἰς αἰῶνα αἰῶνος, τὰ κρίματα Κυρίου ἀληθινὰ δεδικαιωμένα ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτό·

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 18:10 — The fear of the Lord is pure, enduring for ever and ever: the judgments of the Lord are true, [and] justified altogether.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "standing for ever" (עוֹמֶדֶת לָעַד) becomes "enduring for ever and ever" (διαμένων εἰς αἰῶνα αἰῶνος).

**Notes.**

"Fear of the LORD" stands in a series with law, testimony, statutes, commandment and judgments (19:7–9), so it names the law as the thing feared. [inferred]

<a id="ps-33-4"></a>

**Claim.** The word of the LORD is right (יָשָׁר); and all his works are done in
truth. By the word of the LORD were the heavens made.


**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Purity and truth, Efficacy · **Object:** the word of the LORD

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 33:4 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּֽי־יָשָׁר דְּבַר־יְהוָה וְכָל־מַעֲשֵׂהוּ בֶּאֱמוּנָֽה׃</span>
>
> Psalm 33:6 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">בִּדְבַר יְהוָה שָׁמַיִם נַעֲשׂוּ וּבְרוּחַ פִּיו כָּל־צְבָאָֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 33:4 — For the word of the LORD [is] right; and all his works [are done] in truth.
>
> Psalm 33:6 — By the word of the LORD were the heavens made; and all the host of them by the breath of his mouth.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 32:4 — Ὅτι εὐθὴς ὁ λόγος τοῦ Κυρίου, καὶ πάντα τὰ ἔργα αὐτοῦ ἐν πίστει.
>
> Psalm 32:6 — Τῷ λόγῳ τοῦ Κυρίου οἱ οὐρανοὶ ἐστερεώθησαν, καὶ τῷ πνεύματι τοῦ στόματος αὐτοῦ πᾶσα ἡ δύναμις αὐτῶν.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 32:4 — For the word of the Lord is right; and all his works are faithful.
>
> Psalm 32:6 — By the word of the Lord the heavens were established; and all the host of them by the breath of his [mouth].

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "right" is εὐθής, and in 33:6 "were made" is "were established" (ἐστερεώθησαν).

**Notes.**

The word that is "right" is also the creating word. Psalm 33:11 (the
counsel of the LORD stands for ever) is a separate entry.

### Psalm 33:11

<a id="ps-33-11"></a>

**Claim.** The counsel of the LORD standeth for ever.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** the counsel of the LORD

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 33:11 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">עֲצַת יְהוָה לְעוֹלָם תַּעֲמֹד מַחְשְׁבוֹת לִבּוֹ לְדֹר וָדֹֽר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 33:11 — The counsel of the LORD standeth for ever, the thoughts of his heart to all generations.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 32:11 — Ἡ δὲ βουλὴ τοῦ Κυρίου εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα μένει, λογισμοὶ τῆς καρδίας αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ γενεῶν εἰς γενεάς.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 32:11 — But the counsel of the Lord endures for ever, the thoughts of his heart from generation to generation.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "stands" (תַּעֲמֹד) becomes "abides" (μένει), the verb of Isaiah 40:8 in the Greek and of 1 Peter 1:23–25.

**Notes.**

About God's purpose rather than his spoken or written word; included because the Greek puts it in the same terms as the word passages.

### Psalm 78:1-7

<a id="ps-78-5"></a>

**Claim.** He established a testimony in Jacob and appointed a law in Israel, which
he commanded our fathers that they should make known to their children,
"that the generation to come might know them, even the children which
should be born".


**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Transmission to later generations · **Object:** a testimony and a law established in Jacob

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 78:1 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מַשְׂכִּיל לְאָסָף הַאֲזִינָה עַמִּי תּוֹרָתִי הַטּוּ אָזְנְכֶם לְאִמְרֵי־פִֽי׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אֶפְתְּחָה בְמָשָׁל פִּי אַבִּיעָה חִידוֹת מִנִּי־קֶֽדֶם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:3 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אֲשֶׁר שָׁמַעְנוּ וַנֵּדָעֵם וַאֲבוֹתֵינוּ סִפְּרוּ־לָֽנוּ׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:4 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לֹא נְכַחֵד ׀ מִבְּנֵיהֶם לְדוֹר אַחֲרוֹן מְֽסַפְּרִים תְּהִלּוֹת יְהוָה וֶעֱזוּזוֹ וְנִפְלְאוֹתָיו אֲשֶׁר עָשָֽׂה׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:5 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיָּקֶם עֵדוּת ׀ בְּֽיַעֲקֹב וְתוֹרָה שָׂם בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל אֲשֶׁר צִוָּה אֶת־אֲבוֹתֵינוּ לְהוֹדִיעָם לִבְנֵיהֶֽם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:6 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְמַעַן יֵדְעוּ ׀ דּוֹר אַחֲרוֹן בָּנִים יִוָּלֵדוּ יָקֻמוּ וִֽיסַפְּרוּ לִבְנֵיהֶֽם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 78:7 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְיָשִׂימוּ בֵֽאלֹהִים כִּסְלָם וְלֹא יִשְׁכְּחוּ מַֽעַלְלֵי־אֵל וּמִצְוֺתָיו יִנְצֹֽרוּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 78:1 — Maschil of Asaph. Give ear, O my people, [to] my law: incline your ears to the words of my mouth.
>
> Psalm 78:2 — I will open my mouth in a parable: I will utter dark sayings of old:
>
> Psalm 78:3 — Which we have heard and known, and our fathers have told us.
>
> Psalm 78:4 — We will not hide [them] from their children, shewing to the generation to come the praises of the LORD, and his strength, and his wonderful works that he hath done.
>
> Psalm 78:5 — For he established a testimony in Jacob, and appointed a law in Israel, which he commanded our fathers, that they should make them known to their children:
>
> Psalm 78:6 — That the generation to come might know [them, even] the children [which] should be born; [who] should arise and declare [them] to their children:
>
> Psalm 78:7 — That they might set their hope in God, and not forget the works of God, but keep his commandments:

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 77:1 — Συνέσεως τῷ Ἀσάφ. Προσέχετε λαός μου τὸν νόμον μου, κλίνατε τὸ οὖς ὑμῶν εἰς τὰ ῥήματα τοῦ στόματός μου.
>
> Psalm 77:2 — Ἀνοίξω ἐν παραβολαῖς τὸ στόμα μου, φθέγξομαι προβλήματα ἀπʼ ἀρχῆς.
>
> Psalm 77:3 — Ὅσα ἠκούσαμεν καὶ ἔγνωμεν αὐτὰ, καὶ οἱ πατέρες ἡμῶν διηγήσαντο ἡμῖν.
>
> Psalm 77:4 — Οὐκ ἐκρύβη ἀπὸ τῶν τέκνων αὐτῶν εἰς γενεὰν ἑτέραν, ἀπαγγέλλοντες τὰς αἰνέσεις Κυρίου καὶ τὰς δυναστείας αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὰ θαυμάσια αὐτοῦ ἃ ἐποίησε.
>
> Psalm 77:5 — Καὶ ἀνέστησε μαρτύριον ἐν Ἰακὼβ, καὶ νόμον ἔθετο ἐν Ἰσραήλ· ὃν ἐνετείλατο τοῖς πατράσιν ἡμῶν, γνωρίσαι αὐτὸν τοῖς υἱοῖς αὐτῶν,
>
> Psalm 77:6 — ὅπως ἂν γνῷ γενεὰ ἑτέρα, υἱοὶ οἱ τεχθησόμενοι, καὶ ἀναστήσονται καὶ ἀπαγγελοῦσιν αὐτὰ τοῖς υἱοῖς αὐτῶν·
>
> Psalm 77:7 — ἵνα θῶνται ἐπὶ τὸν Θεὸν τὴν ἐλπίδα αὐτῶν, καὶ μὴ ἐπιλάθωνται τῶν ἔργων τοῦ Θεοῦ, καὶ τὰς ἐντολὰς αὐτοῦ ἐκζητήσωσιν.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 77:1 — [A Psalm] of instruction for Asaph. Give heed, O my people, to my law: incline your ear to the words of my mouth.
>
> Psalm 77:2 — I will open my mouth in parables: I will utter dark sayings [which have been] from the beginning.
>
> Psalm 77:3 — All which we have heard and known, and our fathers have declared to us.
>
> Psalm 77:4 — They were not hid from their children to a second generation; [the fathers] declaring the praises of the Lord, and his mighty acts, and his wonders which he wrought.
>
> Psalm 77:5 — And he raised up a testimony in Jacob, and appointed a law in Israel, which he commanded our fathers, to make it known to their children:
>
> Psalm 77:6 — that another generation might know, even the sons which should be born; and they should arise and declare them to their children.
>
> Psalm 77:7 — That they might set their hope on God, and not forget the works of God, but diligently seek his commandments.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same in substance. In 78:7 the Greek reads "seek out his commandments"
(ἐκζητήσωσιν) for "keep" (יִנְצֹרוּ).

**Notes.**

The fullest statement of transmission by teaching across four generations
("our fathers … their children … the generation to come … their
children"). The means is telling, not writing.

### Psalm 89:34-35

<a id="ps-89-34"></a>

**Claim.** My covenant will I not break, nor alter the thing that is gone out of my lips. Once have I sworn by my holiness that I will not lie unto David.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** the covenant and "the thing that is gone out of my lips"

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 89:35 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לֹא־אֲחַלֵּל בְּרִיתִי וּמוֹצָא שְׂפָתַי לֹא אֲשַׁנֶּֽה׃</span>
>
> Psalm 89:36 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אַחַת נִשְׁבַּעְתִּי בְקָדְשִׁי אִֽם־לְדָוִד אֲכַזֵּֽב׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 89:34 — My covenant will I not break, nor alter the thing that is gone out of my lips.
>
> Psalm 89:35 — Once have I sworn by my holiness that I will not lie unto David.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 88:35 — οὐδὲ μὴ βεβηλώσω τὴν διαθήκην μου, καὶ τὰ ἐκπορευόμενα διὰ τῶν χειλέων μου οὐ μὴ ἀθετήσω.
>
> Psalm 88:36 — Ἅπαξ ὤμοσα ἐν τῷ ἁγίῳ μου, εἰ τῷ Δαυὶδ ψεύσομαι.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 88:35 — Neither will I by any means profane my covenant; and I will not make void the things that proceed out of my lips.
>
> Psalm 88:36 — Once have I sworn by my holiness, that I will not lie to David.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "I will not alter" (לֹא אֲשַׁנֶּה) becomes "I will not set aside" (οὐ μὴ ἀθετήσω). "I will not lie" is אֲכַזֵּב in the Hebrew, ψεύσομαι in the Greek.

**Notes.**

God speaking in the first person within the psalm (89:19–37), about his oath to David.

### Psalm 102:18

<a id="ps-102-18"></a>

**Claim.** This shall be written for the generation to come.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Inscripturation · **Object:** this (the record of God's deliverance)

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 102:19 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">תִּכָּתֶב זֹאת לְדוֹר אַחֲרוֹן וְעַם נִבְרָא יְהַלֶּל־יָֽהּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 102:18 — This shall be written for the generation to come: and the people which shall be created shall praise the LORD.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 101:19 — Γραφήτω αὕτη εἰς γενεὰν ἑτέραν, καὶ λαὸς ὁ κτιζόμενος αἰνέσει τὸν Κύριον.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 101:19 — Let this be written for another generation; and the people that shall be created shall praise the Lord.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; the Greek is a jussive, "let this be written for another generation" (Γραφήτω).

### Psalm 105:8; 1 Chronicles 16:15

<a id="ps-105-8"></a>

**Claim.** He hath remembered his covenant for ever, the word which he commanded to a thousand generations.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** the covenant, "the word which he commanded"

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 105:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">זָכַר לְעוֹלָם בְּרִיתוֹ דָּבָר צִוָּה לְאֶלֶף דּֽוֹר׃</span>
>
> 1 Chronicles 16:15 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">זִכְרוּ לְעוֹלָם בְּרִיתוֹ דָּבָר צִוָּה לְאֶלֶף דּֽוֹר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 105:8 — He hath remembered his covenant for ever, the word [which] he commanded to a thousand generations.
>
> 1 Chronicles 16:15 — Be ye mindful always of his covenant; the word [which] he commanded to a thousand generations;

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 104:8 — Ἐμνήσθη εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα διαθήκης αὐτοῦ, λόγου οὗ ἐνετείλατο εἰς χιλίας γενεὰς,
>
> 1 Chronicles 16:15 — Μνημονεύωμεν εἰς αἰῶνα διαθήκης αὐτοῦ, λόγον αὐτοῦ ὃν ἐνετείλατο εἰς χιλίας γενεὰς,

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 104:8 — He has remembered his covenant for ever, the word which he commanded for a thousand generations:
>
> 1 Chronicles 16:15 — Let us remember his covenant for ever, his word which he commanded to a thousand generations,

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "word" is λόγος in both Greek places. In 1 Chronicles the people are told to remember (μνημονεύωμεν, "let us remember").

### Psalm 110:4

<a id="ps-110-4"></a>

**Claim.** The LORD hath sworn, and will not repent, Thou art a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** ground · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** God's oath

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 110:4 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">נִשְׁבַּע יְהוָה ׀ וְלֹא יִנָּחֵם אַתָּֽה־כֹהֵן לְעוֹלָם עַל־דִּבְרָתִי מַלְכִּי־צֶֽדֶק׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 110:4 — The LORD hath sworn, and will not repent, Thou [art] a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 109:4 — Ὤμοσε Κύριος καὶ οὐ μεταμεληθήσεται, σὺ ἱερεὺς εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, κατὰ τὴν τάξιν Μελχισεδέκ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 109:4 — The Lord sware, and will not repent, Thou art a priest for ever, after the order of Melchisedec.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same ("will not repent", וְלֹא יִנָּחֵם / οὐ μεταμεληθήσεται).

**Used in the New Testament:** Hebrews 5:6, Hebrews 6:17-20, Hebrews 7:21, Hebrews 7:28

**Notes.**

An oath God will not take back. Hebrews 6:17-18 and 7:21 build on it the "immutability of his counsel".

### Psalm 111:7-8

<a id="ps-111-7"></a>

**Claim.** All his commandments are sure; they stand fast for ever and ever.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** all his commandments

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 111:7 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מַעֲשֵׂי יָדָיו אֱמֶת וּמִשְׁפָּט נֶאֱמָנִים כָּל־פִּקּוּדָֽיו ׃</span>
>
> Psalm 111:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">סְמוּכִים לָעַד לְעוֹלָם עֲשׂוּיִם בֶּאֱמֶת וְיָשָֽׁר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 111:7 — The works of his hands [are] verity and judgment; all his commandments [are] sure.
>
> Psalm 111:8 — They stand fast for ever and ever, [and are] done in truth and uprightness.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 110:7 — Ἔργα χειρῶν αὐτοῦ, ἀλήθεια καὶ κρίσις· πισταὶ πᾶσαι αἱ ἐντολαὶ αὐτοῦ,
>
> Psalm 110:8 — ἐστηριγμέναι εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα τοῦ αἰῶνος, πεποιημέναι ἐν ἀληθείᾳ καὶ εὐθύτητι.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 110:7 — The works of his hands are truth and judgment: all his commandments are sure:
>
> Psalm 110:8 — established for ever and ever, done in truth and uprightness.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "upheld for ever and ever" (סְמוּכִים לָעַד לְעוֹלָם) becomes "established for ever and ever" (ἐστηριγμέναι).

### Psalm 117:2

<a id="ps-117-2"></a>

**Claim.** The truth of the LORD endureth for ever.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** the truth of the LORD

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 117:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּי גָבַר עָלֵינוּ ׀ חַסְדּוֹ וֶֽאֱמֶת־יְהוָה לְעוֹלָם הַֽלְלוּ־יָֽהּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 117:2 — For his merciful kindness is great toward us: and the truth of the LORD [endureth] for ever. Praise ye the LORD.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 116:2 — Ὅτι ἐκραταιώθη τὸ ἔλεος αὐτοῦ ἐφʼ ἡμᾶς, καὶ ἡ ἀλήθεια τοῦ Κυρίου μένει εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 116:2 — For his mercy has been abundant toward us: and the truth of the Lord endures for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew has no verb ("the truth of the LORD is for ever"); the Greek supplies "abides" (μένει), again the verb of Isaiah 40:8 in the Greek.

### Psalm 119:43, 111

<a id="ps-119-43"></a>

**Claim.** "Take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth"; "thy testimonies have I taken as an heritage for ever."

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Withdrawal, Transmission to later generations · **Object:** the word of truth; thy testimonies

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 119:43 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְֽאַל־תַּצֵּל מִפִּי דְבַר־אֱמֶת עַד־מְאֹד כִּי לְמִשְׁפָּטֶךָ יִחָֽלְתִּי׃</span>
>
> Psalm 119:111 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">נָחַלְתִּי עֵדְוֺתֶיךָ לְעוֹלָם כִּֽי־שְׂשׂוֹן לִבִּי הֵֽמָּה׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 119:43 — And take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth; for I have hoped in thy judgments.
>
> Psalm 119:111 — Thy testimonies have I taken as an heritage for ever: for they [are] the rejoicing of my heart.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 118:43 — Καὶ μὴ περιέλῃς ἐκ τοῦ στόματός μου λόγον ἀληθείας ἕως σφόδρα, ὅτι ἐπὶ τοῖς κρίμασί σου ἐπήλπισα.
>
> Psalm 118:111 — Ἐκληρονόμησα τὰ μαρτύριά σου εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, ὅτι ἀγαλλιάμα τῆς καρδίας μού εἰσιν.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 118:43 — And take not the word of truth utterly out of my mouth; for I have hoped in thy judgments.
>
> Psalm 118:111 — I have inherited thy testimonies for ever; for they are the joy of my heart.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "take not … out of my mouth" is μὴ περιέλῃς, "do not strip away".

**Notes.**

A prayer that the word may not be taken from one's mouth, which
presupposes that it can be; and the word held as an inheritance "for ever"
(לְעוֹלָם), passed down like land.

### Psalm 119:89-91

<a id="ps-119-89"></a>

**Claim.** For ever, O LORD, thy word is settled in heaven.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** thy word

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 119:89 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְעוֹלָם יְהוָה דְּבָרְךָ נִצָּב בַּשָּׁמָֽיִם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 119:90 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְדֹר וָדֹר אֱמֽוּנָתֶךָ כּוֹנַנְתָּ אֶרֶץ וַֽתַּעֲמֹֽד׃</span>
>
> Psalm 119:91 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְֽמִשְׁפָּטֶיךָ עָמְדוּ הַיּוֹם כִּי הַכֹּל עֲבָדֶֽיךָ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 119:89 — For ever, O LORD, thy word is settled in heaven.
>
> Psalm 119:90 — Thy faithfulness [is] unto all generations: thou hast established the earth, and it abideth.
>
> Psalm 119:91 — They continue this day according to thine ordinances: for all [are] thy servants.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 118:89 — Εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, Κύριε, ὁ λόγος σου διαμένει ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ,
>
> Psalm 118:90 — εἰς γενεὰν καὶ γενεὰν ἡ ἀλήθειά σου· ἐθεμελίωσας τὴν γῆν καὶ διαμένει.
>
> Psalm 118:91 — Τῇ διατάξει σου διαμένει ἡμέρα, ὅτι τὰ σύμπαντα δοῦλα σά.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 118:89 — Thy word, O Lord, abides in heaven for ever.
>
> Psalm 118:90 — Thy truth [endures] to all generations; thou hast founded the earth, and it abides.
>
> Psalm 118:91 — The day continues by thy arrangement; for all things are thy servants.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "stands firm" (נִצָּב) becomes "abides" (διαμένει), and the Greek repeats διαμένει of the earth (v. 90) and the day (v. 91).

**Notes.**

The word is said to stand "in heaven". The verse locates the word's
permanence with God, not in a copy on earth. **[inferred]**

### Psalm 119:140

<a id="ps-119-140"></a>

**Claim.** Thy word is very pure.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Purity and truth · **Object:** thy word

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 119:140 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">צְרוּפָה אִמְרָתְךָ מְאֹד וְֽעַבְדְּךָ אֲהֵבָֽהּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 119:140 — Thy word [is] very pure: therefore thy servant loveth it.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 118:140 — Πεπυρωμένον τὸ λόγιόν σου σφόδρα, καὶ ὁ δοῦλός σου ἠγάπησεν αὐτό.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 118:140 — Thy word [has been] very fully tried; and thy servant loves it.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "refined" (צְרוּפָה) is the Greek "tried in the fire" (πεπυρωμένον), as in Psalm 18:30.

**Notes.**

Cited with Psalm 12:6 by the Westminster Larger Catechism (Q. 4) for the purity of Scripture.

### Psalm 119:142, 144

<a id="ps-119-142"></a>

**Claim.** Thy righteousness is an everlasting righteousness, and thy law is the
truth; the righteousness of thy testimonies is everlasting.


**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence, Purity and truth · **Object:** thy law; thy testimonies

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 119:142 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">צִדְקָתְךָ צֶדֶק לְעוֹלָם וְֽתוֹרָתְךָ אֱמֶֽת׃</span>
>
> Psalm 119:144 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">צֶדֶק עֵדְוֺתֶיךָ לְעוֹלָם הֲבִינֵנִי וְאֶחְיֶֽה׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 119:142 — Thy righteousness [is] an everlasting righteousness, and thy law [is] the truth.
>
> Psalm 119:144 — The righteousness of thy testimonies [is] everlasting: give me understanding, and I shall live.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 118:142 — Ἡ δικαιοσύνη σου δικαιοσύνη εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, καὶ ὁ νόυος σου ἀλήθεια.
>
> Psalm 118:144 — Δικαιοσύνη τὰ μαρτύριά σου εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα· συνέτισόν με, καὶ ζήσομαι.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 118:142 — Thy righteousness is an everlasting righteousness, and thy law is truth.
>
> Psalm 118:144 — Thy testimonies [are] an everlasting righteousness: instruct me, and I shall live.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "everlasting" is εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα in both verses.

**Notes.**

The *tsade* strophe, between 119:140 and 119:152.

### Psalm 119:152, 160

<a id="ps-119-152"></a>

**Claim.** Thy testimonies thou hast founded for ever; every one of thy righteous judgments endureth for ever.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** thy testimonies; thy righteous judgments

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 119:152 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">קֶדֶם יָדַעְתִּי מֵעֵדֹתֶיךָ כִּי לְעוֹלָם יְסַדְתָּֽם׃</span>
>
> Psalm 119:160 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">רֹאשׁ־דְּבָרְךָ אֱמֶת וּלְעוֹלָם כָּל־מִשְׁפַּט צִדְקֶֽךָ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 119:152 — Concerning thy testimonies, I have known of old that thou hast founded them for ever.
>
> Psalm 119:160 — Thy word [is] true [from] the beginning: and every one of thy righteous judgments [endureth] for ever.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 118:152 — Κατʼ ἀρχὰς ἔγνων ἐκ τῶν μαρτυρίων σου, ὅτι εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα ἐθεμελίωσας αὐτά.
>
> Psalm 118:160 — Ἀρχὴ τῶν λόγων σου ἀλήθεια, καὶ εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα πάντα τὰ κρίματα τῆς δικαιοσύνης σου.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 118:152 — I have known of old concerning thy testimonies, that thou hast founded them for ever.
>
> Psalm 118:160 — The beginning of thy words is truth; and all the judgments of thy righteousness [endure] for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

Close. In 160 the Hebrew "the sum (רֹאשׁ, head) of thy word is truth"
(KJV "from the beginning") is the Greek "the beginning (Ἀρχὴ) of thy words
is truth".

### Psalm 138:2

<a id="ps-138-2"></a>

**Claim.** Thou hast magnified thy word above all thy name.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Exaltation · **Object:** thy word (Hebrew only)

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 138:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אֶשְׁתַּחֲוֶה אֶל־הֵיכַל קָדְשְׁךָ וְאוֹדֶה אֶת־שְׁמֶךָ עַל־חַסְדְּךָ וְעַל־אֲמִתֶּךָ כִּֽי־הִגְדַּלְתָּ עַל־כָּל־שִׁמְךָ אִמְרָתֶֽךָ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 138:2 — I will worship toward thy holy temple, and praise thy name for thy lovingkindness and for thy truth: for thou hast magnified thy word above all thy name.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 137:2 — Προσκυνήσω πρὸς ναὸν ἅγιόν σου, καὶ ἐξομολογήσομαι τῷ ὀνόματί σου, ἐπὶ τῷ ἐλέει σου καὶ τῇ ἀληθείᾳ σου· ὅτι ἐμεγάλυνας ἐπὶ πᾶν τὸ ὄνομα τὸ ἅγιόν σου.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 137:2 — I will worship toward thy holy temple, and give thanks to thy name, on account of thy mercy and thy truth; for thou hast magnified thy holy name above every thing.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew: "thou hast magnified thy word (אִמְרָתֶךָ) above all thy name."
The Greek: "thou hast magnified thy holy name above every thing"
(ἐμεγάλυνας ἐπὶ πᾶν τὸ ὄνομα τὸ ἅγιόν σου). The Greek has no "word" in the
clause at all. The well-known English reading rests on the Hebrew alone.

### Psalm 145:13

<a id="ps-145-13"></a>

**Claim.** The LORD is faithful in all his words, and holy in all his works: a line missing from the Masoretic Text.

**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** his words (in the line missing from the Hebrew)

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 145:13 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מַֽלְכוּתְךָ מַלְכוּת כָּל־עֹֽלָמִים וּמֶֽמְשֶׁלְתְּךָ בְּכָל־דּוֹר וָדֽוֹר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 145:13 — Thy kingdom [is] an everlasting kingdom, and thy dominion [endureth] throughout all generations.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 144:13 — Ἡ βασιλεία σου βασιλεία πάντων τῶν αἰώνων, καὶ ἡ δεσποτία σου ἐν πάσῃ γενεᾷ καὶ γενεᾷ·

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 144:13 — Thy kingdom is an everlasting kingdom, and thy dominion [endures] through all generations.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

Psalm 145 is an acrostic, each verse beginning with the next letter of
the alphabet, and in the Masoretic Text the line for the letter *nun* is
missing between verses 13 and 14. **[context]** The line is present in the
Psalms Scroll from Qumran (11QPs^a: נאמן אלוהים בדבריו וחסיד בכול
מעשיו, "God is faithful in his words and gracious in all his works") and in
many Greek manuscripts (πιστὸς κύριος ἐν τοῖς λόγοις αὐτοῦ καὶ ὅσιος ἐν
πᾶσι τοῖς ἔργοις αὐτοῦ). It is **absent from Brenton's Greek** as well, which
follows Codex Vaticanus here, so neither text below contains it; the
texts are given to show the gap. The KJV, following the Hebrew, also
lacks it.

**Notes.**

A claim about the faithfulness of God's words that is itself attested
unevenly in the transmitted texts. The companion study
[preservation-hebrew-and-greek.md](../../analysis/preservation-hebrew-and-greek.md#42-before-the-masoretes-several-hebrew-editions)
discusses it as evidence of more than one Hebrew edition of the Psalms.

### Psalm 147:15, 19-20

<a id="ps-147-15"></a>

**Claim.** His word runneth very swiftly. He sheweth his word unto Jacob, his
statutes and his judgments unto Israel. He hath not dealt so with any
nation.


**Speaker:** Psalmist · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Custody, Efficacy · **Object:** his word; his statutes and judgments

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Psalm 147:15 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">הַשֹּׁלֵחַ אִמְרָתוֹ אָרֶץ עַד־מְהֵרָה יָרוּץ דְּבָרֽוֹ׃</span>
>
> Psalm 147:19 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מַגִּיד דברו לְיַעֲקֹב חֻקָּיו וּמִשְׁפָּטָיו לְיִשְׂרָאֵֽל׃</span>
>
> Psalm 147:20 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לֹא עָשָׂה כֵן ׀ לְכָל־גּוֹי וּמִשְׁפָּטִים בַּל־יְדָעוּם הַֽלְלוּ־יָֽהּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Psalm 147:15 — He sendeth forth his commandment [upon] earth: his word runneth very swiftly.
>
> Psalm 147:19 — He sheweth his word unto Jacob, his statutes and his judgments unto Israel.
>
> Psalm 147:20 — He hath not dealt so with any nation: and [as for his] judgments, they have not known them. Praise ye the LORD.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Psalm 147:4 — Ὁ ἀποστέλλων τὸ λόγιον αὐτοῦ τῇ γῇ, ἕως τάχους δραμεῖται ὁ λόγος αὐτοῦ·
>
> Psalm 147:8 — Ἀπαγγέλλων τὸν λόγον αὐτοῦ τῷ Ἰακώβ, δικαιώματα καὶ κρίματα αὐτοῦ τῷ Ἰσραήλ.
>
> Psalm 147:9 — Οὐκ ἐποίησεν οὕτως παντὶ ἔθνει, καὶ τὰ κρίματα αὐτοῦ οὐκ ἐδήλωσεν αὐτοῖς.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Psalm 147:4 — He sends his oracle to the earth: his word will run swiftly.
>
> Psalm 147:8 — He sends his word to Jacob, his ordinances and judgments to Israel.
>
> Psalm 147:9 — He has not done so to any [other] nation; and he has not shewn them his judgments.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Greek counts Psalm 147:12-20 as a separate psalm (Brenton's 147:1-9).
In verse 15 it has τὸ λόγιον αὐτοῦ, "his oracle", then ὁ λόγος αὐτοῦ. In
147:20 the Greek reads "he has not made his judgments known (ἐδήλωσεν) to
them" for "they have not known them".

**Notes.**

The word given to Israel alone, the Old Testament ground of Romans 3:2
("unto them were committed the oracles of God").

### Proverbs 22:19-21

<a id="prov-22-19"></a>

**Claim.** Have not I written to thee excellent things, that I might make thee know
the certainty of the words of truth; that thou mightest answer the words
of truth to them that send unto thee?


**Speaker:** Sage · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Inscripturation, Purity and truth · **Object:** the words of truth

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Proverbs 22:19 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לִהְיוֹת בַּיהוָה מִבְטַחֶךָ הוֹדַעְתִּיךָ הַיּוֹם אַף־אָֽתָּה׃</span>
>
> Proverbs 22:20 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">הֲלֹא כָתַבְתִּי לְךָ שלשום בְּמוֹעֵצֹת וָדָֽעַת׃</span>
>
> Proverbs 22:21 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְהוֹדִֽיעֲךָ קֹשְׁטְ אִמְרֵי אֱמֶת לְהָשִׁיב אֲמָרִים אֱמֶת לְשֹׁלְחֶֽיךָ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Proverbs 22:19 — That thy trust may be in the LORD, I have made known to thee this day, even to thee.
>
> Proverbs 22:20 — Have not I written to thee excellent things in counsels and knowledge,
>
> Proverbs 22:21 — That I might make thee know the certainty of the words of truth; that thou mightest answer the words of truth to them that send unto thee?

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Proverbs 22:19 — Ἵνα σου γένηται ἐπὶ Κύριον ἡ ἐλπὶς, καὶ γνωρίσῃ σοι τὴν ὁδόν σου.
>
> Proverbs 22:20 — Καὶ σὺ δὲ ἀπόγραψαι αὐτὰ σεαυτῷ τρισσῶς, εἰς βουλὴν καὶ γνῶσιν ἐπὶ τὸ πλάτος τῆς καρδίας σου.
>
> Proverbs 22:21 — Διδάσκω οὖν σε ἀληθῆ λόγον, καὶ γνῶσιν ἀγαθὴν ὑπακούειν, τοῦ ἀποκρίνεσθαί σε λόγους ἀληθείας τοῖς προβαλλομένοις σοι.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Proverbs 22:19 — That thy hope may be in the Lord, and he may make thy way known to thee.
>
> Proverbs 22:20 — And do thou too repeatedly record them for thyself on the table of thine heart, for counsel and knowledge.
>
> Proverbs 22:21 — I therefore teach thee truth, and knowledge good to hear; that thou mayest answer words of truth to them that question thee.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Greek differs. For "Have not I written to thee excellent things
(שָׁלִשׁוֹם)?" it has a command to the reader: "And do thou write them for
thyself threefold (ἀπόγραψαι αὐτὰ σεαυτῷ τρισσῶς)". For "that I might make
thee know the certainty of the words of truth" it has "I teach thee
therefore a true word". In the Hebrew the sage writes; in the Greek the
reader is told to copy.

**Notes.**

Westminster Confession 1.1 cites this passage for God's committing his
word to writing. The purpose of the writing is certainty (קֹשְׁטְ, "truth,
certainty").

### Proverbs 30:5-6

<a id="prov-30-5"></a>

**Claim.** Every word of God is pure; add thou not unto his words, lest he reprove thee, and thou be found a liar.

**Speaker:** Sage · **Role:** command · **Categories:** Purity and truth, Prohibition of alteration · **Object:** every word of God

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Proverbs 30:5 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כָּל־אִמְרַת אֱלוֹהַּ צְרוּפָה מָגֵן הוּא לַֽחֹסִים בּֽוֹ׃</span>
>
> Proverbs 30:6 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אַל־תּוֹסְףְּ עַל־דְּבָרָיו פֶּן־יוֹכִיחַ בְּךָ וְנִכְזָֽבְתָּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Proverbs 30:5 — Every word of God [is] pure: he [is] a shield unto them that put their trust in him.
>
> Proverbs 30:6 — Add thou not unto his words, lest he reprove thee, and thou be found a liar.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Proverbs 24:22k — Πάντες γὰρ λόγοι Θεοῦ πεπυρωμένοι, ὑπερασπίζει δὲ αὐτὸς τῶν εὐλαβουμένων αὐτόν.
>
> Proverbs 24:22l — Μὴ προσθῇς τοῖς λόγοις αὐτοῦ, ἵνα μὴ ἐλέγξῃ σε, καὶ ψευδὴς γένῃ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Proverbs 24:22k — For all the words of God are tried in the fire, and he defends those that reverence him.
>
> Proverbs 24:22l — Add not unto his words, lest he reprove thee, and thou be made a liar.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The sense is the same ("all the words of God are tried", πάντες λόγοι
θεοῦ πεπυρωμένοι; "add not to his words", μὴ προσθῇς τοῖς λόγοις αὐτοῦ),
but the Greek places the words of Agur in chapter 24, where Brenton numbers
them 24:22k–l. The Hebrew "God" here is the rare אֱלוֹהַּ.

### Ecclesiastes 12:9-12

<a id="eccl-12-9"></a>

**Claim.** The preacher sought out acceptable words, "and that which was written was
upright, even words of truth"; they "are given from one shepherd". "Of
making many books there is no end."


**Speaker:** Sage · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Inscripturation, Origin · **Object:** words of truth written uprightly

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Ecclesiastes 12:9 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְיֹתֵר שֶׁהָיָה קֹהֶלֶת חָכָם עוֹד לִמַּד־דַּעַת אֶת־הָעָם וְאִזֵּן וְחִקֵּר תִּקֵּן מְשָׁלִים הַרְבֵּֽה׃</span>
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">בִּקֵּשׁ קֹהֶלֶת לִמְצֹא דִּבְרֵי־חֵפֶץ וְכָתוּב יֹשֶׁר דִּבְרֵי אֱמֶֽת׃</span>
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:11 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">דִּבְרֵי חֲכָמִים כַּדָּרְבֹנוֹת וּֽכְמַשְׂמְרוֹת נְטוּעִים בַּעֲלֵי אֲסֻפּוֹת נִתְּנוּ מֵרֹעֶה אֶחָֽד׃</span>
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:12 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְיֹתֵר מֵהֵמָּה בְּנִי הִזָּהֵר עֲשׂוֹת סְפָרִים הַרְבֵּה אֵין קֵץ וְלַהַג הַרְבֵּה יְגִעַת בָּשָֽׂר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Ecclesiastes 12:9 — And moreover, because the preacher was wise, he still taught the people knowledge; yea, he gave good heed, and sought out, [and] set in order many proverbs.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:10 — The preacher sought to find out acceptable words: and [that which was] written [was] upright, [even] words of truth.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:11 — The words of the wise [are] as goads, and as nails fastened [by] the masters of assemblies, [which] are given from one shepherd.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:12 — And further, by these, my son, be admonished: of making many books [there is] no end; and much study [is] a weariness of the flesh.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Ecclesiastes 12:9 — Καὶ περισσὸν ὅτι ἐγένετο Ἐκκλησιαστὴς σοφὸς, ὅτι ἐδίδαξε γνῶσιν σὺν τὸν ἄνθρωπον, καὶ οὖς ἐξιχνιάσεται κόσμιον παραβολῶν.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:10 — Πολλὰ ἐζήτησεν Ἐκκλησιαστὴς τοῦ εὑρεῖν λόγους θελήματος, καὶ γεγραμμένον εὐθύτητος, λόγους ἀληθείας.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:11 — Λόγοι σοφῶν ὡς τὰ βούκεντρα, καὶ ὡς ἧλοι πεφυτευμένοι, οἳ παρὰ τῶν συνθεμάτων ἐδόθησαν ἐκ ποιμένος ἑνός.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:12 — Καὶ περισσὸν ἐξ αὐτῶν υἱέ μου φύλαξαι· τοῦ ποιῆσαι βιβλία πολλὰ οὐκ ἔστι περασμὸς, καὶ μελέτη πολλὴ κόπωσις σαρκός.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Ecclesiastes 12:9 — And because the Preacher was wise above [others, so it was] that he taught man excellent knowledge, and the ear will trace out the parables.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:10 — The Preacher sought diligently to find out acceptable words, and a correct writing, [even] words of truth.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:11 — The words of the wise are as goads, and as nails firmly fastened, which have been given from one shepherd by agreement.
>
> Ecclesiastes 12:12 — And moreover, my son, guard thyself by means of them: of making many books there is no end; and much study is a weariness of the flesh.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same; "given from one shepherd" is ἐδόθησαν ἐκ ποιμένος ἑνός.

**Notes.**

The writer's labour in seeking, arranging and writing is set beside the
claim that the words come "from one shepherd". The warning against "many
books" sets the given words apart from the endless writing of men.

### Isaiah 8:16, 20

<a id="isa-8-16"></a>

**Claim.** Bind up the testimony, seal the law among my disciples. To the law and to the testimony; if they speak not according to this word, it is because there is no light in them.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Custody · **Object:** the testimony and the law

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 8:16 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">צוֹר תְּעוּדָה חֲתוֹם תּוֹרָה בְּלִמֻּדָֽי׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 8:20 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לְתוֹרָה וְלִתְעוּדָה אִם־לֹא יֹֽאמְרוּ כַּדָּבָר הַזֶּה אֲשֶׁר אֵֽין־לוֹ שָֽׁחַר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 8:16 — Bind up the testimony, seal the law among my disciples.
>
> Isaiah 8:20 — To the law and to the testimony: if they speak not according to this word, [it is] because [there is] no light in them.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 8:16 — Τότε φανεροὶ ἔσονται οἱ σφραγιζόμενοι τὸν νόμον τοῦ μὴ μαθεῖν.
>
> Isaiah 8:20 — Νόμον γὰρ εἰς βοήθειαν ἔδωκεν, ἵνα εἴπωσιν οὐχ ὡς τὸ ῥῆμα τοῦτο, περὶ οὗ οὐκ ἔστι δῶρα δοῦναι περὶ αὐτοῦ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 8:16 — Then shall those who seal themselves that they may not learn the law be made manifest.
>
> Isaiah 8:20 — For he has given the law for a help, that they should not speak according to this word, concerning which there are no gifts to give for it.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The two texts have different meanings. The Hebrew of 8:16 commands the
testimony to be bound and sealed "among my disciples" (בְּלִמֻּדָי). The
Greek reads "Then shall those who seal up the law, that they may not learn
it, be made manifest." In 8:20 the Greek has "he has given the law for a
help", not the Hebrew's appeal "to the law and to the testimony".

**Notes.**

Westminster cites 8:20 (WCF 1.1, 1.8; WLC Q. 3) for the appeal to Scripture; that reading depends on the Hebrew.

### Isaiah 9:8

<a id="isa-9-8"></a>

**Claim.** The Lord sent a word into Jacob, and it hath lighted (fallen) upon Israel.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Efficacy · **Object:** a word sent into Jacob

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 9:7 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">דָּבָר שָׁלַח אֲדֹנָי בְּיַעֲקֹב וְנָפַל בְּיִשְׂרָאֵֽל׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 9:8 — ¶ The Lord sent a word into Jacob, and it hath lighted upon Israel.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 9:7 — Θάνατον ἀπέστειλε Κύριος ἐπὶ Ἰακώβ, καὶ ἦλθεν ἐπὶ Ἰσραήλ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 9:7 — The Lord has sent death upon Jacob, and it has come upon Israel.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew consonants דבר are pointed דָּבָר, "a word". The Greek read the
same letters as דֶּבֶר, "pestilence": "The Lord sent death (θάνατον) upon
Jacob." A preservation passage that exists only on one vocalization of
the consonantal text.

**Notes.**

The word "falls" on Israel (נָפַל), the opposite of the idiom in which
God's word does not fall to the ground; here it falls by landing on its
target.

### Isaiah 14:24, 27; 44:26

<a id="isa-14-24"></a>

**Claim.** "As I have purposed, so shall it stand"; "who shall disannul it?"; he
"confirmeth the word of his servant, and performeth the counsel of his
messengers".


**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** God's purpose; the word of his servant

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 14:24 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">נִשְׁבַּע יְהוָה צְבָאוֹת לֵאמֹר אִם־לֹא כַּאֲשֶׁר דִּמִּיתִי כֵּן הָיָתָה וְכַאֲשֶׁר יָעַצְתִּי הִיא תָקֽוּם׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 14:27 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּֽי־יְהוָה צְבָאוֹת יָעָץ וּמִי יָפֵר וְיָדוֹ הַנְּטוּיָה וּֽמִי יְשִׁיבֶֽנָּה׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 44:26 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מֵקִים דְּבַר עַבְדּוֹ וַעֲצַת מַלְאָכָיו יַשְׁלִים הָאֹמֵר לִירוּשָׁלִַם תּוּשָׁב וּלְעָרֵי יְהוּדָה תִּבָּנֶינָה וְחָרְבוֹתֶיהָ אֲקוֹמֵֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 14:24 — ¶ The LORD of hosts hath sworn, saying, Surely as I have thought, so shall it come to pass; and as I have purposed, [so] shall it stand:
>
> Isaiah 14:27 — For the LORD of hosts hath purposed, and who shall disannul [it]? and his hand [is] stretched out, and who shall turn it back?
>
> Isaiah 44:26 — That confirmeth the word of his servant, and performeth the counsel of his messengers; that saith to Jerusalem, Thou shalt be inhabited; and to the cities of Judah, Ye shall be built, and I will raise up the decayed places thereof:

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 14:24 — Τάδε λέγει Κύριος σαβαὼθ, ὃν τρόπον εἴρηκα, οὕτως ἔσται, καὶ ὃν τρόπον βεβούλευμαι, οὕτως μενεῖ,
>
> Isaiah 14:27 — Ἃ γὰρ ὁ Θεὸς ὁ ἅγιος βεβούλευται, τίς διασκεδάσει; καὶ τὴν χεῖρα αὐτοῦ τὴν ὑψηλὴν τίς ἀποστρέψει;
>
> Isaiah 44:26 — καὶ ἱστῶν ῥὴμα παιδὸς αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὴν βουλὴν τῶν ἀγγέλων αὐτοῦ ἀληθεύων· ὁ λέγων τῇ Ἱερουσαλὴμ, κατοικηθήσῃ, καὶ ταῖς πόλεσι τῆς Ἰδουμαίας, οἰκοδομηθήσεσθε, καὶ τὰ ἔρημα αὐτῆς ἀνατελεῖ·

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 14:24 — Thus saith the Lord of hosts, As I have said, so it shall be: and as I have purposed, so [the matter] shall remain:
>
> Isaiah 14:27 — For what the Holy God has purposed, who shall frustrate? and who shall turn back his uplifted hand?
>
> Isaiah 44:26 — and confirming the word of his servant, and verifying the counsel of his messengers: who says to Jerusalem, Thou shalt be inhabited; and to the cities of Idumea, Ye shall be built, and her desert places shall spring forth.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Greek of 14:24 has no oath ("Thus saith the Lord of hosts" for "The
LORD of hosts hath sworn") and renders "so shall it stand" (תָקוּם) as "so
shall it abide" (μενεῖ), the verb of Isaiah 40:8 in the Greek. In 44:26
"confirmeth the word" is ἱστῶν ῥῆμα, "establishing the word".

**Notes.**

The word of the prophet ("his servant", "his messengers") is made to stand (מֵקִים) by God.

### Isaiah 29:10-14

<a id="isa-29-11"></a>

**Claim.** The vision is become as the words of a sealed book that neither learned
nor unlearned can read; "their fear toward me is taught by the precept of
men".


**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** warning · **Categories:** Withdrawal, Corruption among men · **Object:** the vision of all; the fear of God taught by men

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 29:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּֽי־נָסַךְ עֲלֵיכֶם יְהוָה רוּחַ תַּרְדֵּמָה וַיְעַצֵּם אֶת־עֵֽינֵיכֶם אֶת־הַנְּבִיאִים וְאֶת־רָאשֵׁיכֶם הַחֹזִים כִּסָּֽה׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 29:11 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַתְּהִי לָכֶם חָזוּת הַכֹּל כְּדִבְרֵי הַסֵּפֶר הֶֽחָתוּם אֲשֶֽׁר־יִתְּנוּ אֹתוֹ אֶל־יוֹדֵעַ הספר לֵאמֹר קְרָא נָא־זֶה וְאָמַר לֹא אוּכַל כִּי חָתוּם הֽוּא׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 29:12 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְנִתַּן הַסֵּפֶר עַל אֲשֶׁר לֹֽא־יָדַע סֵפֶר לֵאמֹר קְרָא נָא־זֶה וְאָמַר לֹא יָדַעְתִּי סֵֽפֶר׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 29:13 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיֹּאמֶר אֲדֹנָי יַעַן כִּי נִגַּשׁ הָעָם הַזֶּה בְּפִיו וּבִשְׂפָתָיו כִּבְּדוּנִי וְלִבּוֹ רִחַק מִמֶּנִּי וַתְּהִי יִרְאָתָם אֹתִי מִצְוַת אֲנָשִׁים מְלֻמָּדָֽה׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 29:14 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">לָכֵן הִנְנִי יוֹסִף לְהַפְלִיא אֶת־הָֽעָם־הַזֶּה הַפְלֵא וָפֶלֶא וְאָֽבְדָה חָכְמַת חֲכָמָיו וּבִינַת נְבֹנָיו תִּסְתַּתָּֽר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 29:10 — For the LORD hath poured out upon you the spirit of deep sleep, and hath closed your eyes: the prophets and your rulers, the seers hath he covered.
>
> Isaiah 29:11 — And the vision of all is become unto you as the words of a book that is sealed, which [men] deliver to one that is learned, saying, Read this, I pray thee: and he saith, I cannot; for it [is] sealed:
>
> Isaiah 29:12 — And the book is delivered to him that is not learned, saying, Read this, I pray thee: and he saith, I am not learned.
>
> Isaiah 29:13 — ¶ Wherefore the Lord said, Forasmuch as this people draw near [me] with their mouth, and with their lips do honour me, but have removed their heart far from me, and their fear toward me is taught by the precept of men:
>
> Isaiah 29:14 — Therefore, behold, I will proceed to do a marvellous work among this people, [even] a marvellous work and a wonder: for the wisdom of their wise [men] shall perish, and the understanding of their prudent [men] shall be hid.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 29:10 — ὅτι πεπότικεν ὑμᾶς Κύριος πνεύματι κατανύξεως, καὶ καμμύσει τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς αὐτῶν, καὶ τῶν προφητῶν αὐτῶν, καὶ τῶν ἀρχόντων αὐτῶν, οἱ ὁρῶντες τὰ κρυπτά.
>
> Isaiah 29:11 — Καὶ ἔσται ὑμῖν τὰ ῥήματα πάντα ταῦτα, ὡς οἱ λόγοι τοῦ βιβλίου τοῦ ἐσφραγισμένου τσύτου, ὃ ἐὰν δῶσιν αὐτὸ ἀνθρώπῳ ἐπισταμένῳ γράμματα, λέγοντες, ἀνάγνωθι ταῦτα· καὶ ἐρεῖ, οὐ δύναμαι ἀναγνῶναι, ἐσφράγισται γάρ.
>
> Isaiah 29:12 — καὶ δοθήσεται τὸ βιβλίον τοῦτο εἰς χεῖρας ἀνθρώπου μὴ ἐπισταμένου γράμματα, καὶ ἐρεῖ αὐτῷ, ἀνάγνωθι τοῦτο· καὶ ἐρεῖ, οὐκ ἐπίσταμαι γράμματα.
>
> Isaiah 29:13 — Καὶ εἶπε Κύριος, ἐγγίζει μοι ὁ λαὸς οὗτος ἐν τῷ στόματι αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἐν τοῖς χείλεσιν αὐτῶν τιμῶσί με, ἡ δὲ καρδία αὐτῶν πόῤῥω ἀπέχει ἀπʼ ἐμοῦ· μάτην δὲ σέβονταί με, διδάσκοντες ἐντάλματα ἀνθρώπων καὶ διδασκαλίας.
>
> Isaiah 29:14 — Διατοῦτο ἰδοὺ προσθήσω τοῦ μεταθεῖναι τὸν λαὸν τοῦτον, καὶ μεταθήσω αὐτούς, καὶ ἀπολῶ τὴν σοφίαν τῶν σοφῶν, καὶ τὴν σύνεσιν τῶν συνετῶν κρύψω.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 29:10 — For the Lord has made you to drink a spirit of deep sleep; and he shall close their eyes, and [the eyes] of their prophets and of their rulers, who see secret things.
>
> Isaiah 29:11 — And all these things shall be to you as the words of this sealed book, which if they shall give to a learned man, saying, Read this, he shall then say, I cannot read [it], for it is sealed.
>
> Isaiah 29:12 — And this book shall be given into the hands of a man that is unlearned, and [one] shall say to him, Read this; and he shall say, I am not learned.
>
> Isaiah 29:13 — And the Lord has said, This people draw nigh to me with their mouth, and they honour me with their lips, but their heart is far from me: but in vain do they worship me, teaching the commandments and doctrines of men.
>
> Isaiah 29:14 — Therefore behold I will proceed to remove this people, and I will remove them: and I will destroy the wisdom of the wise, and will hide the understanding of the prudent.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Greek of 29:11 reads "all these words" for "the vision of all", and
29:13 adds "in vain do they worship me, teaching the commandments and
doctrines of men" (μάτην δὲ σέβονταί με …). Jesus quotes 29:13 in that
Greek form.

**Used in the New Testament:** Matthew 15:8-9, Mark 7:6-7, Romans 11:8, 1 Corinthians 1:19

**Notes.**

The word is present as a book but closed to its readers, a judgment of
blindness rather than a loss of the text. Verse 13 is the passage Jesus
applies to the tradition that "made the word of God of none effect"
([Matthew 15:6](#matt-15-6)).

### Isaiah 30:8

<a id="isa-30-8"></a>

**Claim.** Write it in a table, note it in a book, that it may be for the time to come for ever and ever.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** means · **Categories:** Inscripturation · **Object:** the prophecy against trust in Egypt

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 30:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">עַתָּה בּוֹא כָתְבָהּ עַל־לוּחַ אִתָּם וְעַל־סֵפֶר חֻקָּהּ וּתְהִי לְיוֹם אַחֲרוֹן לָעַד עַד־עוֹלָֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 30:8 — ¶ Now go, write it before them in a table, and note it in a book, that it may be for the time to come for ever and ever:

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 30:8 — Νῦν οὖν καθίσας γράψον ἐπὶ πυξίου ταῦτα καὶ εἰς βιβλίον, ὅτι ἔσται εἰς ἡμέρας ταῦτα καιρῷ, καὶ ἕως εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 30:8 — Now then sit down and write these words on a tablet, and in a book; for these things shall be for [many long] days, and even for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew consonants לעד are pointed in the Masoretic Text as לָעַד, "for
ever", giving "for the latter day, for ever, to eternity". Read as לְעֵד, the
same letters mean "for a witness", which many modern versions adopt
**[context]**. The Greek has "for many days, and even for ever" (εἰς
ἡμέρας … καὶ ἕως εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα). The verse is an example of a reading that
turns on vowels the consonantal text does not carry.

### Isaiah 31:2

<a id="isa-31-2"></a>

**Claim.** He will bring evil, and will not call back his words.

**Speaker:** Moses or a prophet, for God · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** his words

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 31:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וְגַם־הוּא חָכָם וַיָּבֵא רָע וְאֶת־דְּבָרָיו לֹא הֵסִיר וְקָם עַל־בֵּית מְרֵעִים וְעַל־עֶזְרַת פֹּעֲלֵי אָֽוֶן׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 31:2 — Yet he also [is] wise, and will bring evil, and will not call back his words: but will arise against the house of the evildoers, and against the help of them that work iniquity.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 31:2 — Καὶ αὐτὸς σοφῶς ἦγεν ἐπʼ αὐτοὺς κακά, καὶ ὁ λόγος αὐτοῦ οὐ μὴ ἀθετηθῇ, καὶ ἐπαναστήσεται ἐπʼ οἴκους ἀνθρώπων πονηρῶν, καὶ ἐπὶ τὴν ἐλπίδα αὐτῶν τὴν ματαίαν,

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 31:2 — Therefore he has wisely brought evils upon them, and his word shall not be frustrated; and he shall rise up against the houses of wicked men, and against their vain hope,

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "has not removed (or turned aside) his words" (לֹא הֵסִיר) becomes "his word shall not be set aside" (οὐ μὴ ἀθετηθῇ).

### Isaiah 34:16

<a id="isa-34-16"></a>

**Claim.** Seek ye out of the book of the LORD, and read; no one of these shall fail.

**Speaker:** Moses or a prophet, for God · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment, Inscripturation · **Object:** the book of the LORD

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 34:16 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">דִּרְשׁוּ מֵֽעַל־סֵפֶר יְהוָה וּֽקְרָאוּ אַחַת מֵהֵנָּה לֹא נֶעְדָּרָה אִשָּׁה רְעוּתָהּ לֹא פָקָדוּ כִּֽי־פִי הוּא צִוָּה וְרוּחוֹ הוּא קִבְּצָֽן׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 34:16 — ¶ Seek ye out of the book of the LORD, and read: no one of these shall fail, none shall want her mate: for my mouth it hath commanded, and his spirit it hath gathered them.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 34:16 — Ἀριθμῷ παρῆλθον, καὶ μία αὐτῶν οὐκ ἀπώλετο· ἑτέρα τὴν ἑτέραν οὐκ ἐζήτησαν, ὅτι ὁ Κύριος αὐτοῖς ἐνετείλατο, καὶ τὸ πνεῦμα αὐτοῦ συνήγαγεν αὐτά.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 34:16 — They passed by in [full] number, and not one of them perished: they sought not one another; for the Lord commanded them, and his Spirit gathered them.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew tells the reader to "seek out of the book of the LORD, and
read". The Greek has no reference to a book: "They passed by in number,
and not one of them perished." Only the Hebrew contains the appeal to a
written book.

### Isaiah 40:6-8

<a id="isa-40-8"></a>

**Claim.** The grass withereth, the flower fadeth; but the word of our God shall stand for ever.

**Speaker:** Heavenly voice · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Permanence · **Object:** the word of our God

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 40:6 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">קוֹל אֹמֵר קְרָא וְאָמַר מָה אֶקְרָא כָּל־הַבָּשָׂר חָצִיר וְכָל־חַסְדּוֹ כְּצִיץ הַשָּׂדֶֽה׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 40:7 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">יָבֵשׁ חָצִיר נָבֵֽל צִיץ כִּי רוּחַ יְהוָה נָשְׁבָה בּוֹ אָכֵן חָצִיר הָעָֽם׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 40:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">יָבֵשׁ חָצִיר נָבֵֽל צִיץ וּדְבַר־אֱלֹהֵינוּ יָקוּם לְעוֹלָֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 40:6 — The voice said, Cry. And he said, What shall I cry? All flesh [is] grass, and all the goodliness thereof [is] as the flower of the field:
>
> Isaiah 40:7 — The grass withereth, the flower fadeth: because the spirit of the LORD bloweth upon it: surely the people [is] grass.
>
> Isaiah 40:8 — The grass withereth, the flower fadeth: but the word of our God shall stand for ever.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 40:6 — Φωνὴ λέγοντος, βόησον· καὶ εἶπα, τί βοήσω; πᾶσα σὰρξ χορτὸς, καὶ πᾶσα δόξα ἀνθρώπου ὡς ἄνθος χόρτου·
>
> Isaiah 40:7 — Ἐξηράνθη ὁ χόρτος, καὶ τὸ ἄνθος ἐξέπεσε·
>
> Isaiah 40:8 — τὸ δὲ ῥῆμα τοῦ Θεοῦ ἡμῶν μένει εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 40:6 — The voice of one saying, Cry; and I said, What shall I cry? All flesh is grass, and all the glory of man as the flower of grass.
>
> Isaiah 40:7 — The grass withers, and the flower fades:
>
> Isaiah 40:8 — but the word of our God abides for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

- **Length.** The Greek lacks most of verse 7 ("because the spirit of the
  LORD bloweth upon it: surely the people is grass") and the repetition
  at the start of verse 8. It reads simply: "the grass withered, and the
  flower fell; but the word of our God abides for ever."
- **Verse 6.** The Hebrew "all its goodliness" (חַסְדּוֹ) is the Greek "all
  the glory of man" (πᾶσα δόξα ἀνθρώπου).
- **The verb.** The Hebrew "shall stand" (יָקוּם) is the Greek "abides"
  (μένει).

**Used in the New Testament:** 1 Peter 1:24-25

**Notes.**

The voice of 40:6 is unidentified ("the voice said, Cry"), within an oracle
introduced as God's (40:1). 1 Peter 1:24–25 quotes the Greek, not the
Hebrew: it has the shorter text, "glory", and "abides". See the
1 Peter entry for how the Greek New Testament editions differ on this.

### Isaiah 45:23

<a id="isa-45-23"></a>

**Claim.** I have sworn by myself, the word is gone out of my mouth in righteousness, and shall not return.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** the word gone out of God's mouth

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 45:23 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">בִּי נִשְׁבַּעְתִּי יָצָא מִפִּי צְדָקָה דָּבָר וְלֹא יָשׁוּב כִּי־לִי תִּכְרַע כָּל־בֶּרֶךְ תִּשָּׁבַע כָּל־לָשֽׁוֹן׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 45:23 — I have sworn by myself, the word is gone out of my mouth [in] righteousness, and shall not return, That unto me every knee shall bow, every tongue shall swear.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 45:23 — Κατʼ ἐμαυτοῦ ὀμνύω, εἰ μὴ ἐξελεύσεται ἐκ τοῦ στόματός μου δικαιοσύνη, οἱ λόγοι μου οὐκ ἀποστραφήσονται· Ὅτι ἐμοὶ κάμψει πᾶν γόνυ, καὶ ὀμεῖται πᾶσα γλῶσσα τὸν Θεὸν,

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 45:23 — By myself I swear, righteousness shall surely proceed out of my mouth; my words shall not be frustrated; that to me every knee shall bend, and every tongue shall swear by God,

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew: "a word in righteousness has gone out of my mouth, and shall
not return" (וְלֹא יָשׁוּב). The Greek divides it: "righteousness shall go
out of my mouth; my words shall not be turned back" (οἱ λόγοι μου οὐκ
ἀποστραφήσονται).

**Used in the New Testament:** Romans 14:11, Philippians 2:10-11

**Notes.**

The New Testament quotes the second half (every knee shall bow), not the clause about the word.

### Isaiah 46:10-11

<a id="isa-46-10"></a>

**Claim.** My counsel shall stand; I have spoken it, I will also bring it to pass.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** my counsel; what I have spoken

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 46:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">מַגִּיד מֵֽרֵאשִׁית אַחֲרִית וּמִקֶּדֶם אֲשֶׁר לֹא־נַעֲשׂוּ אֹמֵר עֲצָתִי תָקוּם וְכָל־חֶפְצִי אֶעֱשֶֽׂה׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 46:11 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">קֹרֵא מִמִּזְרָח עַיִט מֵאֶרֶץ מֶרְחָק אִישׁ עצתו אַף־דִּבַּרְתִּי אַף־אֲבִיאֶנָּה יָצַרְתִּי אַף־אֶעֱשֶֽׂנָּה׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 46:10 — Declaring the end from the beginning, and from ancient times [the things] that are not [yet] done, saying, My counsel shall stand, and I will do all my pleasure:
>
> Isaiah 46:11 — Calling a ravenous bird from the east, the man that executeth my counsel from a far country: yea, I have spoken [it], I will also bring it to pass; I have purposed [it], I will also do it.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 46:10 — ἀναγγέλλων πρότερον τὰ ἔσχατα πρὶν γενέσθαι, καὶ ἅμα συνετελέσθη· καὶ εἶπα, πᾶσα ἡ βουλή μου στήσεται, καὶ πάντα ὅσα βεβούλευμαι, ποιήσω·
>
> Isaiah 46:11 — Καλῶν ἀπὸ ἀνατολῶν πετεινὸν, καὶ ἀπὸ γῆς πόῤῥωθεν περὶ ὧν βεβούλευμαι, ἐλάλησα, καὶ ἤγαγον, ἔκτισα καὶ ἐποίησα, ἤγαγον αὐτὸν, καὶ εὐώδωσα τὴν ὁδὸν αὐτοῦ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 46:10 — telling beforehand the latter events before they come to pass, and they are accomplished together: and I said, All my counsel shall stand, and I will do all things that I have planned:
>
> Isaiah 46:11 — calling a bird from the east, and from a land afar off, for the things which I have planned: I have spoken, and brought [him]; I have created and made [him]; I have brought him, and prospered his way.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The same in substance; the Greek "all my counsel shall stand" (πᾶσα ἡ βουλή μου στήσεται).

### Isaiah 55:10-11

<a id="isa-55-11"></a>

**Claim.** My word shall not return unto me void, but shall accomplish that which I please.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment, Efficacy · **Object:** my word that goeth forth out of my mouth

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 55:10 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כִּי כַּאֲשֶׁר יֵרֵד הַגֶּשֶׁם וְהַשֶּׁלֶג מִן־הַשָּׁמַיִם וְשָׁמָּה לֹא יָשׁוּב כִּי אִם־הִרְוָה אֶת־הָאָרֶץ וְהוֹלִידָהּ וְהִצְמִיחָהּ וְנָתַן זֶרַע לַזֹּרֵעַ וְלֶחֶם לָאֹכֵֽל׃</span>
>
> Isaiah 55:11 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כֵּן יִֽהְיֶה דְבָרִי אֲשֶׁר יֵצֵא מִפִּי לֹֽא־יָשׁוּב אֵלַי רֵיקָם כִּי אִם־עָשָׂה אֶת־אֲשֶׁר חָפַצְתִּי וְהִצְלִיחַ אֲשֶׁר שְׁלַחְתִּֽיו׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 55:10 — For as the rain cometh down, and the snow from heaven, and returneth not thither, but watereth the earth, and maketh it bring forth and bud, that it may give seed to the sower, and bread to the eater:
>
> Isaiah 55:11 — So shall my word be that goeth forth out of my mouth: it shall not return unto me void, but it shall accomplish that which I please, and it shall prosper [in the thing] whereto I sent it.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 55:10 — Ὡς γὰρ ἂν καταβῇ ὁ ὑετὸς ἢ χιὼν ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ, καὶ οὐ μὴ ἀποστραφῇ ἕως ἂν μεθύσῃ τὴν γῆν, καὶ ἐκτέκῃ, καὶ ἐκβλαστήσῃ, καὶ δῷ σπέρμα τῷ σπείροντι, καὶ ἄρτον εἰς βρῶσιν·
>
> Isaiah 55:11 — οὕτως ἔσται τὸ ῥῆμά μου, ὃ ἐὰν ἐξέλθῃ ἐκ τοῦ στόματός μου, οὐ μὴ ἀποστραφῇ, ἕως ἂν τελεσθῇ ὅσα ἂν ἠθέλησα, καὶ εὐοδώσω τὰς ὁδούς σου, καὶ τὰ ἐντάλματά μου.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 55:10 — For as rain shall come down, or snow, from heaven, and shall not return until it have saturated the earth, and it bring forth, and bud, and give seed to the sower, and bread for food:
>
> Isaiah 55:11 — so shall my word be, whatever shall proceed out of my mouth, it shall by no means turn back, until all the things which I willed shall have been accomplished; and I will make thy ways prosperous, and [will effect] my commands.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew "shall not return to me empty" (לֹא־יָשׁוּב אֵלַי רֵיקָם); Brenton's
Greek has "shall not return (οὐ μὴ ἀποστραφῇ) until all that I willed is
accomplished", without "empty", and adds "and I will prosper thy ways and
my commandments".

**Notes.**

A claim about the word's effect in the world, not about its text.

### Isaiah 59:21

<a id="isa-59-21"></a>

**Claim.** My words which I have put in thy mouth shall not depart out of thy mouth, nor out of the mouth of thy seed, nor out of the mouth of thy seed's seed, from henceforth and for ever.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Transmission to later generations · **Object:** my spirit and my words put in thy mouth

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Isaiah 59:21 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַאֲנִי זֹאת בְּרִיתִי אוֹתָם אָמַר יְהוָה רוּחִי אֲשֶׁר עָלֶיךָ וּדְבָרַי אֲשֶׁר־שַׂמְתִּי בְּפִיךָ לֹֽא־יָמוּשׁוּ מִפִּיךָ וּמִפִּי זַרְעֲךָ וּמִפִּי זֶרַע זַרְעֲךָ אָמַר יְהוָה מֵעַתָּה וְעַד־עוֹלָֽם׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Isaiah 59:21 — As for me, this [is] my covenant with them, saith the LORD; My spirit that [is] upon thee, and my words which I have put in thy mouth, shall not depart out of thy mouth, nor out of the mouth of thy seed, nor out of the mouth of thy seed’s seed, saith the LORD, from henceforth and for ever.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Isaiah 59:21 — Καὶ αὕτη αὐτοῖς ἡ παρʼ ἐμοῦ διαθήκη, εἶπε Κύριος· τὸ πνεῦμα τὸ ἐμὸν, ὅ ἐστιν ἐπὶ σοί, καὶ τὰ ῥήματα, ἃ ἔδωκα εἰς τὸ στόμα σου, οὐ μὴ ἐκλίπῃ ἐκ τοῦ στόματός σου, καὶ ἐκ τοῦ στόματος τοῦ σπέρματός σου· εἶπε γὰρ Κύριος ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν καὶ εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Isaiah 59:21 — And this shall be my covenant with them, said the Lord; My Spirit which is upon thee, and the words which I have put in thy mouth, shall never fail from thy mouth, nor from the mouth of thy seed, for the Lord has spoken it, henceforth and for ever.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

- The Hebrew verb is plural, "they shall not depart" (לֹא־יָמוּשׁוּ, the
  spirit and the words together); the Greek is singular (οὐ μὴ ἐκλίπῃ).
- The Greek lacks "nor out of the mouth of thy seed's seed".
- The Greek attaches "from henceforth and for ever" to "for the Lord has
  said it" (εἶπε γὰρ Κύριος ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν καὶ εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα).

**Used in the New Testament:** Romans 11:26-27

**Notes.**

The strongest Old Testament promise of preservation in transmission: the
words are to remain in the mouths of a succession of generations. It is a
covenant promise to the redeemed of Zion (59:20); Romans 11:27 quotes the
covenant clause. It speaks of words in mouths, not of a written copy.

### Jeremiah 1:12

<a id="jer-1-12"></a>

**Claim.** I will hasten (watch over) my word to perform it.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** claim · **Categories:** Fulfilment · **Object:** my word

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Jeremiah 1:12 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וַיֹּאמֶר יְהוָה אֵלַי הֵיטַבְתָּ לִרְאוֹת כִּֽי־שֹׁקֵד אֲנִי עַל־דְּבָרִי לַעֲשֹׂתֽוֹ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Jeremiah 1:12 — Then said the LORD unto me, Thou hast well seen: for I will hasten my word to perform it.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Jeremiah 1:12 — Καὶ εἶπε Κύριος πρὸς μὲ, καλῶς ἑώρακας, διότι ἐγρήγορα ἐγὼ ἐπὶ τοὺς λόγους μου τοῦ ποιῆσαι αὐτούς.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Jeremiah 1:12 — And the Lord said to me, Thou hast well seen: for I have watched over my words to perform them.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew is a pun: the prophet sees an almond rod (שָׁקֵד) and God says he
is watching (שֹׁקֵד) over his word. The Greek has "a rod of walnut"
(καρυΐνην) and "I have watched" (ἐγρήγορα); the pun is lost.

### Jeremiah 8:8

<a id="jer-8-8"></a>

**Claim.** The law of the LORD is with us? Lo, the lying pen of the scribes has made it into a lie.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** warning · **Categories:** Corruption among men · **Object:** the law of the LORD in the scribes' hands

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Jeremiah 8:8 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">אֵיכָה תֹֽאמְרוּ חֲכָמִים אֲנַחְנוּ וְתוֹרַת יְהוָה אִתָּנוּ אָכֵן הִנֵּה לַשֶּׁקֶר עָשָׂה עֵט שֶׁקֶר סֹפְרִֽים׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Jeremiah 8:8 — How do ye say, We [are] wise, and the law of the LORD [is] with us? Lo, certainly in vain made he [it;] the pen of the scribes [is] in vain.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Jeremiah 8:8 — Πῶς ἐρεῖτε, ὅτι σοφοί ἐσμεν ἡμεῖς, καὶ νόμος Κυρίου μεθʼ ἡμῶν ἐστιν; εἰς μάτην ἐγενήθη σχοῖνος ψευδὴς γραμματεῦσιν.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Jeremiah 8:8 — How will ye say, We are wise, and the law of the Lord is with us? In vain have the scribes used a false pen.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

The Hebrew: "Surely, behold, the lying pen of the scribes has wrought
falsely" (לַשֶּׁקֶר עָשָׂה עֵט שֶׁקֶר סֹפְרִים). The Greek: "In vain have the
scribes used a false pen" (εἰς μάτην ἐγενήθη σχοῖνος ψευδὴς
γραμματεῦσιν; σχοῖνος is a reed or measuring line). The KJV's "in vain
made he it; the pen of the scribes is in vain" follows a different
construal.

**Notes.**

Scripture's own acknowledgement that the written law could be falsified
by those charged with it. Whether the charge is of altering the text or
of false teaching from it is disputed. **[context]**

### Jeremiah 23:28-29, 36

<a id="jer-23-28"></a>

**Claim.** He that hath my word, let him speak my word faithfully; is not my word like as a fire? Ye have perverted the words of the living God.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** warning · **Categories:** Corruption among men, Efficacy · **Object:** my word

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Jeremiah 23:28 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">הַנָּבִיא אֲשֶׁר־אִתּוֹ חֲלוֹם יְסַפֵּר חֲלוֹם וַאֲשֶׁר דְּבָרִי אִתּוֹ יְדַבֵּר דְּבָרִי אֱמֶת מַה־לַתֶּבֶן אֶת־הַבָּר נְאֻם־יְהוָֽה׃</span>
>
> Jeremiah 23:29 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">הֲלוֹא כֹה דְבָרִי כָּאֵשׁ נְאֻם־יְהוָה וּכְפַטִּישׁ יְפֹצֵֽץ סָֽלַע׃</span>
>
> Jeremiah 23:36 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">וּמַשָּׂא יְהוָה לֹא תִזְכְּרוּ־עוֹד כִּי הַמַּשָּׂא יִֽהְיֶה לְאִישׁ דְּבָרוֹ וַהֲפַכְתֶּם אֶת־דִּבְרֵי אֱלֹהִים חַיִּים יְהוָה צְבָאוֹת אֱלֹהֵֽינוּ׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Jeremiah 23:28 — The prophet that hath a dream, let him tell a dream; and he that hath my word, let him speak my word faithfully. What [is] the chaff to the wheat? saith the LORD.
>
> Jeremiah 23:29 — [Is] not my word like as a fire? saith the LORD; and like a hammer [that] breaketh the rock in pieces?
>
> Jeremiah 23:36 — And the burden of the LORD shall ye mention no more: for every man’s word shall be his burden; for ye have perverted the words of the living God, of the LORD of hosts our God.

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Jeremiah 23:28 — Ὁ προφήτης ἐν ᾧ τὸ ἐνύπνιόν ἐστι, διηγησάσθω τὸ ἐνύπνιον αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἐν ᾧ ὁ λόγος μου πρὸς αὐτὸν, διηγησάσθω τὸν λόγον μου ἐπʼ ἀληθείας· τί τὸ ἄχυρον πρὸς τὸν σῖτον; οὕτως οἱ λόγοι μου, λέγει Κύριος.
>
> Jeremiah 23:29 — Οὐκ ἰδοὺ οἱ λόγοι μου, ὥσπερ πῦρ, λέγει Κύριος, καὶ ὡς πέλυξ κόπτων πέτραν;
>
> Jeremiah 23:36 — Καὶ λῆμμα Κυρίου μὴ ὀνομάζετε ἔτι, ὅτι τὸ λῆμμα τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ ἔσται ὁ λόγος αὐτοῦ.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Jeremiah 23:28 — The prophet who has a dream, let him tell his dream; and [he] in whom is my word [spoken] to him, let him tell my word truly: what is the chaff to the corn? so are my words, saith the Lord.
>
> Jeremiah 23:29 — Behold, are not my words as fire? saith the Lord; and as an axe cutting the rock?
>
> Jeremiah 23:36 — And do ye name no more the burden of the Lord; for his own word shall be a man's burden.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**

- In 23:28 the Greek adds "so are my words" after "what is the chaff to
  the wheat?"
- In 23:36 the Greek lacks the last clause, "for ye have perverted
  (וַהֲפַכְתֶּם) the words of the living God, of the LORD of hosts our God".
  **[context]** The Greek Jeremiah is roughly one-seventh shorter than the
  Hebrew and arranges the book differently; Hebrew fragments from
  Qumran (4QJer^b,d) agree with the shorter form.

### Jeremiah 26:2

<a id="jer-26-2"></a>

**Claim.** Speak all the words that I command thee; diminish not a word.

**Speaker:** God, through a prophet · **Role:** command · **Categories:** Prohibition of alteration · **Object:** all the words I command thee

**Hebrew (Masoretic Text, Westminster Leningrad Codex)**

> Jeremiah 26:2 — <span dir="rtl" lang="he">כֹּה ׀ אָמַר יְהוָה עֲמֹד בַּחֲצַר בֵּית־יְהוָה וְדִבַּרְתָּ עַל־כָּל־עָרֵי יְהוּדָה הַבָּאִים לְהִשְׁתַּחֲוֺת בֵּית־יְהוָה אֵת כָּל־הַדְּבָרִים אֲשֶׁר צִוִּיתִיךָ לְדַבֵּר אֲלֵיהֶם אַל־תִּגְרַע דָּבָֽר׃</span>

**English of the Hebrew (KJV, 1769)**

> Jeremiah 26:2 — Thus saith the LORD; Stand in the court of the LORD’s house, and speak unto all the cities of Judah, which come to worship in the LORD’s house, all the words that I command thee to speak unto them; diminish not a word:

**Greek (Septuagint, Brenton's edition)**

> Jeremiah 33:2 — Οὕτως εἶπε Κύριος, στῆθι ἐν αὐλῇ οἴκου Κυρίου, καὶ χρηματιεῖς ἅπασι τοῖς Ἰουδαίοις, καὶ πᾶσι τοῖς ἐρχομένοις προσκυνεῖν ἐν οἴκῳ Κυρίου, ἅπαντας τοὺς λόγους οὓς συνέταξά σοι χρηματίσαι αὐτοῖς, μὴ ἀφέλῃς ῥῆμα.

**English of the Greek (Brenton, 1851)**

> Jeremiah 33:2 — Thus said the Lord; Stand in the court of the Lord's house, and thou shalt declare to all the Jews, and to all that come to worship in the house of the Lord, all the words which I commanded thee to speak to them; abate not one word.

**Hebrew and Greek compared.**
